Both institutions therefore would often ignore the most basic\nstandards and procedural guarantees.1354 Common mistakes included:\n     v Court decisions are based on outdated laws;\n     v The application for a detention order is not handed out to the person concerned and is not\n         translated;\n     v An interpreter has to be present at the court hearing and they must have sufficient language skills\n         both in the language of the person concerned and in German. This is not always taken care of in\n         practice.\n\nBecause these standards were often ignored, an estimated 50% of complaints to higher courts were\nsuccessful and the detention orders issued by the local courts were found to be unlawful, according to\nthe activist (see also below). Other sources seem to confirm that local courts often do not sufficiently\nexamine whether the detention order is necessary and proportionate and it has been further reported that\nbasic procedural standards are sometimes violated.1355 The Federal Supreme Court has therefore\nfrequently ruled such detention orders as unlawful. According to the lawyer Peter Fahlbusch, this occurred\nin around two thirds of all cases brought before the Federal Supreme Court in 2021.1356 Recent decisions\nof the Federal Supreme Court in which a detention order was ruled unlawful include cases where:\n     v The detention order was not given by a judge but by an executive authority without due\n         justification of not awaiting a court order; 1357\n     v A lawyer was not given the opportunity to attend a hearing;1358\n     v Authorities had not given sufficient reasons to justify the duration of detention. According to Peter Fahlbusch,\nthe firm represented 2,458 clients who were in detention pending removal between 2001 and October\n2023. In 1,283 of these cases (52.2%), courts found detention orders to be unlawful. For the clients\naffected, this had resulted in about four weeks of detention on average (25.8 days). The quota had not been reached in practice because lengthy processing by local authorities resulted in fewer than 1,000 applications per month.", "question_ids": ["6B.2", "6B.3", "6B.7", "8B.2", "8B.6"], "polarity": "adverse", "period_fit": "The restriction and administrative shortfall remained operative in 2023.", "ruler_attribution": "This describes federal law and intergovernmental implementation during Scholz's chancellorship; personal authorship is not established.", "limitations": ["The quota was ultimately fulfilled in 2023, but the source attributes earlier underuse to procedural delays."], "start_unit": 222, "end_unit": 223, "locator": "page 222 through page 223", "exact_excerpt": "Another discussion arose in 2022 on the additional criteria for family reunification in cases where minor\nchildren are the sponsors and want to reunite with their parents. Parents of unaccompanied minors may\nonly be granted a visa if the family already existed in the country of origin.1470 In the case discussed, the\nchild was born in Germany, so it was argued that the ‘family’ did not exist yet at the time the parents were\nin the country of origin. However, the Higher Administrative Court decided that the criterion of the ‘already\nexisting family’ does not necessarily require identical persons but that the family already exists as family\ntribe, meaning that the child does not need to be born prior to their arrival in Germany in order to later\nbecome a sponsor for the parent.1471\n\nIf family members of refugees apply for family reunification later than 3 months after status determination\nhas become final, ‘normal rules’ for family reunification apply. In particular, refugees living in Germany\nhave to prove that they can cover the cost of living for themselves and their families and that they have\nsufficient living space.1472 For family reunification of spouses, a further requirement is that both spouses\nhave to be at least 18 years of age.1473\n\nOne important privilege applies regardless of whether the procedure for family reunification is initiated\nwithin the three-month period or at a later date: Spouses of refugees who wish to immigrate to Germany\nby means of family reunification do not have to prove that they have basic German language skills.1474\n\nIn 2023 a total of 22,637 visas for family reunification were issued to beneficiaries of international\nprotection, out of which 10,570 for beneficiaries of refugee protection and 12,067 for beneficiaries of\nsubsidiary protection.1475 The number of visas issued in 2023 was again higher than in 2022 (19,449) and\nin the years 2020 and 2021 when Covid impacted the family reunification procedure,1476 but is still a little\nbelow the 2019 numbers (24,835).1477 4,125 visas for family reunification purposes to refugees were\ngranted to Syrian nationals, 991 to Afghan nationals, 1,776 to Turkish, 361 to Iraqi and 299 to Iranian\nnationals..1478\n\nFamily reunification for beneficiaries of subsidiary protection\n\nIn 2018 the right to family reunification was effectively abolished for beneficiaries of subsidiary\nprotection and was replaced with a provision according to which 1,000 relatives shall be granted a visa\nto enter Germany each month.1479 This means that the privileged conditions that apply to family\nreunification for refugees do not apply to beneficiaries of subsidiary protection and have been replaced\nwith a ‘humanitarian clause’ which places family reunification at the discretion of the authorities. As such,\nthe beneficiary of subsidiary protection does not have a right to family reunification. Instead, the family\nmembers need to apply themselves for reunification and the decision is at the discretion of the authorities.\n\n\n\n\n1470\n       Section 26 (3) (no.2) Asylum Act.\n1471\n       Higher Court of Rhineland-Palatinate, Decision 13 A 11241/21.OVG, 25 June 2022, available in German at:\n       http://bit.ly/3HolJwj.\n1472\n       Sections 27(3) and 29 Residence Act.\n1473\n       Section 30(1)(1) Residence Act.\n1474\n       Section 30(1)(3) Residence Act.\n1475\n       Reply to oral parliamentary question by Clara Bünger (Die Linke), 19 December 2023, question no. 80,\n       available in German at: https://bit.ly/3SOEbnI.\n1476\n       Reply to oral parliamentary question by Clara Bünger (Die Linke), 8 February 2023, question no. 37, available\n       in German at: https://bit.ly/3OJPJaq; for information on the impact of Covid-19 on the family reunification\n       procedure, see: AIDA, Country Report Germany – Update on the year 2021, April 2022, available at\n       https://bit.ly/3XnN7RS, 170.\n1477\n       Neue Osnabrücker Zeitung, ‘Familiennachzug hat im vergangenen Jahr wieder deutlich zugenommen’, 10\n       March 2022, available in German at: https://bit.ly/389cYbi.\n1478\n       Reply to oral parliamentary question by Clara Bünger (Die Linke), 19 December 2023, question no 80, availabl\n       in German at: https://bit.ly/3SOEbnI.\n1479\n       Section 36a Residence Act; Section 104(13) Residence Act.\n\n\n                                                       222\n\nThis is regulated in Section 36a of the Residence Act, according to which only members of the ‘immediate\nfamily’ (spouses, registered partners, minor unmarried children, parents of unaccompanied children) are\neligible for family reunification. In order to be included in the monthly quota of 1,000 visa, ‘humanitarian\nreasons’ shall be decisive, which are listed in the law as follows:\n     v Long duration of separation of family members,\n     v Separation of families with at least one (minor) unmarried child,\n     v Serious risks to life, limb or personal freedom of a family member living abroad,\n     v Serious illness, need for care or serious disabilities of a family member living abroad.\n\nIn addition, the welfare of the child and ‘integration aspects’ (e.g., language skills, ability to provide for\nmeans of living) may be taken into account.1480\n\nThe monthly quota for visa has not been reached since the introduction of the new regulation for\nbeneficiaries of subsidiary protection, due to a complicated procedure involving three different authorities:\nEmbassies or consulates – often in cooperation with IOM – have to carry out an interview with the family\nmembers who have applied for visa; then the local alien’s offices in Germany have to decide whether the\nnecessary humanitarian criteria are fulfilled; and then they have to pass on the visa applications to the\nFederal Administrative Office (Bundesverwaltungsamt) which theoretically should select the most urgent\n1,000 cases per month.1481 In practice, this selection does not take place since procedures at the local\nauthorities are lengthy, resulting in less than 1,000 applications per month. As a result, the Federal\nAdministrative Office usually authorises all cases submitted by the local authorities and informs the\nembassies or consulates that visas may be issued.\n\nUntil 12 December 2023, 12,067 visas were issued to family members of beneficiaries of subsidiary\nprotection.1482 In contrast to the time between August 2018 and April 2021, where only 20,600 visas were\ngranted(62% of the total of 33,000 visas that the law would have foreseen for this period), the quota of\n12,000 per annum is thus fulfilled in 2023.1483 In 2022, 8,900 visas were issued, i.e., 74% of the quota of\n12,000 per year.1484 Around 17,594 requests for appointments at embassies were pending as of June\n2022.1485 Since it is likely that many persons have asked for appointments several times, the actual\nnumber of persons applying for visa for this purpose is likely to be lower.1486 10,778 of those visas in 2023\nwere granted to Syrian nationals, 176 to Afghan, 54 to Turkish, 83 to Iraqi and 2 to Iranian nationals.1487\n\nAlso for beneficiaries of subsidiary protection, the question arises concerning the time of their minority in\nrelation to family reunification. As already mentioned, the German Courts have in the past evaluated the\nage of the beneficiary at the time of the court decision and must now refer to the time of the asylum\napplication due to several CJEU rulings (see above). However, those rulings are based on the directive\n2003/86/EC,1488 which does not apply to beneficiaries of subsidiary protection.1489 Thus, German courts\nhave argued, that the right to family reunification ends when the subsidiary protection status holder\n\n1480\n       Detailed information on the legal requirements and the procedure can be found at: https://familie.asyl.net/.\n1481\n "raw_sha256": "35a16fff9b7b255d989c2a5cc6e56b8de7d67ce65e07a782935d6cac97244469", "fact_summary": "A 2023 BAMF evaluation found that the statutory obligation requiring many recognized refugees to live in assigned locations had no positive effect on integration. It reported that housing shortages prolonged stays in accommodation centres and that the obligation negatively affected labour-market access; the rule had been made permanent in 2019 without the evaluation originally contemplated before extension.", "question_ids": ["5B.7", "5B.8", "6B.2", "6B.3", "8B.7", "8B.8"], "polarity": "adverse", "period_fit": "Institutional evaluation published in 2023, assessing a policy operating during the period.", "ruler_attribution": "The evaluation concerns federal policy and BAMF administration; the source does not show that Scholz personally initiated or altered the residence rule.", "limitations": ["The rule predates Scholz's chancellorship, although its continued operation and evaluation fall within the period."], "start_unit": 227, "end_unit": 228, "locator": "page 227 through page 228", "exact_excerpt": "partners or minor children) live in another place.1514 In 2022 the legal framework for the obligatory place\nof residence has been slightly changed. New exception grounds for the obligation to take up a specific\nplace of residence have been introduced. Accordingly, beneficiaries of international protection may be\nexempted from the obligation if their participation in an integration course or other qualification measures\nrequires them to move somewhere else.\n\nAccording to the official explanatory memorandum, the residence rule is supposed to promote sustainable\nintegration by preventing segregation of communities.1515 However, it has been questioned whether the\nway in which the provision has been put into effect is suitable for achieving the intended aim.1516 A study\nby the Technical University of Dresden on existing ‘residence rules’ was published in March 2018. The\nauthor points out that it will take more time to assess the positive or negative effects of the regulations\nintroduced in 2016. At the same time, she concludes that the new measures should not be expected to\nhave too many regulatory effects on the labour and housing markets and on integration efforts of refugees.\nThis is because the number of persons affected by the new regulations was rather low in comparison to\nthe overall migrant and refugee communities in Germany. Furthermore, she argues that integration\nprocesses are generally difficult to regulate by law.1517\n\nA brief analysis of the impact of the residence rule was published in January 2020.1518 This paper is based\non the ‘IAB-BAMF-SOEP survey’, a representative study on the living conditions of refugees which has\nbeen carried out on an annual basis since 2016. In this analysis, the situation of refugees who are subject\nto the residence rule is compared to other refugees, in particular those that were granted refugee status\nat an earlier date, before the introduction of the regulation. The duration of stay in Germany as well as\nother regional and individual factors were taken into account in order to avoid possible distortions. The\nmain findings of this analysis are:\n     v Refugees who are subject to the residence rule are less likely to be employed;\n     v Refugees who are subject to the residence rule are less likely to live in private accommodation\n         (as opposed to collective accommodation);\n     v It could not be ascertained whether the residence rule had a positive or negative impact on\n         refugees’ German language skills or on their (successful) participation in integration courses.\n\nAn independent study from ‘Paritaetischer Gesamtverband’ from 2022 confirmed these findings. In the\nstudy, the obligation has been highly criticised as standing in contrast to the aim of facilitating integration.\nE.g. access to the job market, access to regular housing and protection for victims of violence is heavily\nimpeded by the obligation.1519\n\nIn a ruling of 4 September 2018, the High Administrative Court of North Rhine-Westphalia decided that\nthe Federal State’s regulation on the residence obligation for refugees was illegal. According to the court,\nthe wording of the directive was too restrictive as it stated that refugees ‘should, as a rule’ be obliged to\nreside in the town or district to which they had been accommodated during the asylum procedure.1520\nAlthough the decision was restricted to North Rhine-Westphalia, it highlights that authorities generally\nhave to conduct an individual assessment to determine whether a residence obligation is useful ’to\nenhance the prospects of a sustainable integration’.1521 In the aftermath of the judgment the government\nof North Rhine-Westphalia generally evaluated the states rules and amended those parts where the court\n\n1514\n       Section 12a(5) Residence Act.\n1515\n       Explanatory memorandum, Bundestag Document no. 18/8614, 42-43.\n1516\n       Clara Schlotheuber and Sebastian Röder, Integrative (?) Das Beispiel Sachsen, MIDEM-\n       Policy Paper 01/18, Dresden, available at: https://bit.ly/3wkFVgN.\n1518\n       Institut für Arbeitsmarkt- und Berufsforschung (IAB): Wohnsitzauflagen reduzieren die Chancen auf\n       Arbeitsmarktintegration, IAB-Kurzbericht 2/2020, January 2020, available in German at: https://bit.ly/34rH7wL.\n1519\n       Der Paritätische Gesamtverband, Die Wohnsitzregelung gem. § 12a AufenthG, April 2022, available in\n       German at: https://bit.ly/3jmhNEq..\n1520\n       High Administrative Court North Rhine-Westphalia, Decision 18 A 256/18, 4 September 2018.\n1521\n       Claudius Voigt, ‘Zum Urteil des OVG Nordrhein-Westfalen: Rechtswidrige pauschale Wohnsitzzuweisung’,\n       Asylmagazin 12/2018, 454-458.\n\n\n                                                        227\n\nobjected.1522 Apart from this ruling, few cases have become known in which courts were asked to decide\non the legality of the residence rule.\n\nThe residence rule for persons with protection status had originally been introduced for a period of three\nyears, so it would have run out at the end of July 2019. The explanatory memorandum to the integration\nact of 2016 had stated that the decision on whether the rule would be discontinued or extended should\nbe based on an evaluation of its impact. Although this evaluation never took place, a new law was\nintroduced in the spring of 2019 and entered into force on 12 July 2019.1523 This law has now made the\nresidence rule permanent. The main principles of the regulation remain unchanged, as only a few\nclarifications were introduced (e.g. concerning the continuation of the residence rule after an authorised\nmove to another Federal State). Furthermore, a new sanction was introduced for persons who have\nmoved to another place without permission while they were subject to the residence rule: In these cases,\nthe obligation to stay in the assigned place of residence can now be extended ‘by the (same) period of\ntime at which the foreigner has not complied with the obligation’.1524 Again, the explanatory memorandum\nto the law states that an evaluation of the impact of Section 12a of the Residence Act is supposed to take\nplace within three years.1525 With the amendments in the legal framework slightly improve the situation of\nbeneficiaries since more exceptions and reasons for lifting the obligation have been introduced.\n\nHowever, according to an evaluation by the BAMF from 2023, the obligation to take up residence in\nspecific does not have a positive effect on integration. To the contrast, due to the general shortcomings\nin housing, the obligation fosters a prolonged stay in accommodation centres, since refugees cannot find\nprivate housing in the assigned places. Furthermore, the obligation to reside in specific places has a\nnegative impact on the access to the labour market.1526\n\n        2. Travel documents\n\nPersons with refugee status are entitled to ‘travel documents for refugees’ (‘Reiseausweis für\nFlüchtlinge’) in accordance with Article 28 of the 1951 Refugee Convention. The travel document for\nrefugees is either automatically issued together with the residence permit after status determination has\nbecome final, or it is issued upon application. The document shall adhere to European standards1527 and\ntherefore has to include a storage medium with the facial image, fingerprints etc.1528\n\nThe duration of the travel document for refugees is ‘up to three years’. For each renewal the refugee has\nto pay a EUR 70 fee.1529 Alternatively, it can be issued as a preliminary travel document, i.e. without an\nelectronic storage medium, for ‘up to one year’.1530 A prolongation of the document is not possible, so\nrefugees have to apply for a new document once the old one has expired. If their travel document expires\non a journey, they may exceptionally apply for a travel document for aliens (see below) from abroad.1531\nIn these cases applicants need a valid residence permit and the embassy checks whether a cessation of\nthe German residence permit due to an absence of more than six months from Germany can be\n\n\n\n\n1522\n       Ministry for children, family, refugees and integration North Rhine-Westphalia, Bericht zur Evaluierung der\n       Wohnsitzregelung für anerkannte Schutzberechtigte in Nordrhein-Westfalen, 1 August 2019, available in\n       German at: http://bit.ly/3Jyhm4E.\n1523\n       Act to remove the time-limit of the integration Act (Gesetz zur Entfristung des Integrationsgesetzes), Official\n       Gazette I, No. 25, 11 July 2019, 914.\n1524\n       Section 12a(1)(3) Residence Act.\n1525\n       Explanatory memorandum to draft bill, 25 March 2019, 19/8692, 9.\n1526\n       BAMF, Evaluation der Wohnsitzregelung nach § 12a AufenthG, 29 August 2023, available in German at:\n       https://bit.ly/49yB2Po.\n1527\n       Council Regulation (EC) No 2252/2004 of 13 December 2004 on standards for security features and\n       biometrics in passports and travel documents issued by Member States, OJ L385/1.\n1528\n       Section 4(4) Residence Regulation (Aufenthaltsverordnung).\n1529\n       Section 48 Residence Regulation.\n1530\n       Section 4(1) Residence Regulation.\n1531\n       Section 4(1) No. 1, Section 5 and Section 7 and Section 11 Regulation on Residence.\n\n\n                                                        228", "excerpt_sha256": "dbf895d1f599a9fdb826f3913421da5f6a69017fd371995d4e5ca1a98b931742", "verification_status": "accepted", "verification_notes": []}, {"evidence_id": "BATCH-0011-R06-E008", "source_id": "SRC-a007cee33f0d9205", "url": "https://asylumineurope.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/06/AIDA-DE_2023-Update.pdf", "title": "Country Report: Germany; The program had previously been reported as useful for information and contact with authorities, but clients also reported insufficient staff capacity and regional availability.", "question_ids": ["6B.1", "6B.2", "6B.4", "6B.9", "8B.2", "8B.3"], "polarity": "mixed", "period_fit": "Budget allocation and implementation evidence for 2023.", "ruler_attribution": "The funding decision is attributed to the Federal Government during Scholz's tenure, not specifically to Scholz.", "limitations": ["The cited client evaluation was from 2015; current 2023 outcome data were not yet available."], "start_unit": 233, "end_unit": 233, "locator": "page 233", "exact_excerpt": "adopted to the needs of beneficiaries of international protection.1560 For 2023 the Federal government\ndecided to spend in total 81,5 million € for the ‘Migration counselling for adult immigrants’.1561 For 2024,\nthe Federal government initially announced severe cuts and wanted to limit the funding to EUR 57 million.\nSocial welfare associations heavily criticised that the cuts in funding stand in contrast to the rising need\ndue to the increased numbers of immigrants in the last years.1562 Following political pressure by the\nopposition and the welfare associations, the funding was raised to EUR 77,5 million for 2024.1563 In 2015,\nten years after its introduction the BAMF, conducted a first study on the impact of the ‘Migration\ncounselling for adult immigrants.1564 Former clients reported that the program provides diversified\ninformation and counselling for different aspects such as labour, access to language classes, access to\nsocial benefits. 46% of former clients mentioned that they needed the counselling for support with forms\nand in contact with public authorities. Clients were mostly satisfied with the counselling, but several\nmentioned the lack of capacities in staff and regional availability. A new study is currently conducted by\nDeZIM (Deutsches Zentrum für Integations- und Migrationsforschung) and presumably will be published\nin 2025.1565\n\nSome Federal States set up additional integration programmes or fund project of private initiatives which\naim at the integration of migrants. North Rhine-Westphalia reformed in 2021 the ‘Act to support social\nparticipation and integration in North Rhine-Westphalia’ (Gesetz zur Förderung der gesellschaftlichen\nTeilhabe und Integration in Nordrhein-Westfalen) by which the state’s government commits itself to invest\nat least € 130,000 per year on integration programmes.1566 For the implementation the state\nreconceptualised ‘municipal centres of integration’ (Kommunale Integrationszentren) which shall\ncoordinate and conceptualise integration programmes tailored to the needs and existing private initiatives\nin the municipalities. As for the Federal programmes, the services are open to migrants in general, but\nsome programmes are specifically tailored to beneficiaries of international protection and people with a\n'tolerated stay’ (Duldung).1567 Berlin already introduced a similar Act in 2010 which was though completely\nrevised in 2021. The ‘Act to support participation in the migration society’ (Gesetz zur Förderung der\nPartizipation in der Migrationsgesellschaft) forsees likewise to support integration programmes but\nadditionally focuses on the diversitfication of the administration in Berlin.1568 According to a study from\n2022, five states (Berlin, Bavaria, Baden-Wuerttemberg, North-Rhine Westphalia and Schleswig-\nHolstein) implemented regional laws on integration and participation.1569 The study concludes that these\nregional laws have been successful if they see integration as a task for the whole society and not only the\nindividual migrant. The advantage of these laws is that processes and actors are streamlined and that the\nlaws have a symbolic function in advocating integration and participation. However, the success depends\na lot on the political will in the different states according to the study.\n\n\n\n\n1560\n       BAMF, Integrationsangebote im Überblick, available at: "raw_sha256": "35a16fff9b7b255d989c2a5cc6e56b8de7d67ce65e07a782935d6cac97244469", "fact_summary": "A 2023 longitudinal analysis found that about 54% of surveyed asylum arrivals obtained employment within six years and up to 62% within seven years. Six years after arrival, employment was 67% for men but 23% for women; 70% had found skilled work, although 41% of employed respondents worked below the qualification level held in their country of origin.", "question_ids": ["5B.10", "5B.8", "6B.10", "6B.2", "6B.3", "6B.8", "8B.7"], "polarity": "mixed", "period_fit": "2023 analysis of integration outcomes, with multi-year cohorts.", "ruler_attribution": "The findings concern refugee integration under German institutions and policies; they do not establish personal causation by Scholz.", "limitations": ["The study did not distinguish respondents by protection or residence status, so it is only indicative for beneficiaries of protection."], "start_unit": 234, "end_unit": 235, "locator": "page 234 through page 235", "exact_excerpt": "Recognition of professional qualifications has been often described as a major practical obstacle for\naccess to the labour market. This does not only affect refugees but other immigrants as well. The main\nreasons identified are the administrative hurdles since the procedure is highly formalised. The first barrier\nis that depending on which qualification should be recognised foreigners need to approach different\nauthorities. Secondly, foreigners need to understand whether the recognition of their qualification is\nmandatory. The recognition is mandatory for third country nationals and independently from the nationality\nfor so called ‘reglemented labour’ (reglementierte Berufe) e.g., teachers, engeneers, health\npractitioners.1570 Moreover the recognition usually requires certificates and additional documents. In case\nforeigners cannot provide these documents, they need to undergo additional tests and contact a\ncounselling person.1571\n\nIn addition to the bureaucratic barriers, the recognitions scheme works largely to the disadvantage of\nrefugee women as their qualifications from the country of origin often do not match the formal\nrequirements for recognitions under German Law.1572 If recognitions take place there is a highly positive\neffect on the income and the formal level of the labour market involvement of migrants in general and\npersons granted a protection status in particular.1573 Studies show a significant gender gap in access to\nthe labour market, employment levels as well as remuneration that is far greater than the ‘usual’ gender\npay gap in Germany.1574 The German government therefore has set up an information portal offering\nadvice on the necessary procedures (‘Recognition in Germany‘). However, the recognition of\nqualifications remains challenging despite its clear positive effects on integration into the labour market\nas well as integration more generally.1575\n\nAvailable official statistics on unemployment only distinguish between nationalities, but not between\nresidence statuses of persons concerned. Therefore, it is not possible to determine how many\nbeneficiaries of international protection have successfully integrated into the labour market.\n\nFor information on how the Covid-19 pandemic impacted the integration of refugees and beneficiaries of\nsubsidiary protection, see AIDA 2022 update.\n\nResearch on labour market integration of refugees over the last decade points to a relatively successful\nintegration in the long run: a ‘brief analysis’ on the integration of refugees into the labour market was\npublished in February 2020 and updated in 2023. It is based on the ‘IAB-BAMF-SOEP-survey’, a long-\nterm study on the living conditions of persons who have come to Germany as asylum seekers between\n2013 and 2019.1576 The main conclusions of the updated study from 2023 include the following:1577\n\n\n\n1570\n       All labour where the scope of practice is defined by law is counted as ‚reglemented labour‘.\n1571\n       On the procedure of recognition of qualifications, see: Bundesagentur für Arbeit, Anerkennung von Abschluss\n       und Zeugnis, available in German at: http://bit.ly/3l4l6jP.\n1572\n       See Kosyakova, Yuliya; Gundacker, Lidwina; Salikutluk, Zerrin; Trübswetter, Parvati (2021):\n       Arbeitsmarktintegration in Deutschland: Geflüchtete Frauen müssen viele Hindernisse überwinden. Six years\n         after arrival 67% of men are employed, whereas only 23% of women have an employment.\n         Reasons for the substantial difference are the unequal distribution of care work for children,\n         unequal financial investments in the language and education, different educational background\n         in the country of origin.\n       v Within 6 six years of their arrival, 70% found ‘skilled labour’. However, compared to their\n         employment in their country of origin, still 41% of the employed persons were employed in job\n         jobs which are less qualified than the one they had in their country of origin.\n\nIt must be noted that this study does not distinguish between the residence status of the persons\nsurveyed. Therefore, it is not clear how many of the persons surveyed have been granted protection\nstatus. Nevertheless, the analysis provides at least an indication for the situation of persons with\nprotection status, since a high percentage of persons who have arrived as asylum seekers between 2013\nand 2016 have been granted protection.\n\nThese findings have been confirmed by the final report on this long-term survey published in November\n2020.1578 The study points to the positive developments triggered through specific integration measures\naiming at labour market integration and show that inclusion into the formal labour market is likely to take\nplace after three to five years of stay. Moreover, the study shows a significant effect of the duration of\nasylum procedures on the labour market integration: If an asylum procedure is prolonged by six months\n(in comparison to the regular duration of such procedures) the chances of labour market integration is\ndiminished by 11%. A positive outcome of the procedure enhances the chances by 30% with the stable\nresidence status being the most influential factor for employment of (former) asylum seekers.1579 The\nresidence requirement of Section 12a of the Residence Act on the other hand has a detrimental effect on\nlabour market integration of refugees even though its purpose was to enhance integration.1580\n\n       2. Access to education\n\nPersons with refugee status and beneficiaries of subsidiary protection are entitled to take up vocational\ntraining as well as school or university education, if they can prove that they have the necessary\nqualifications. They can also receive support for the costs of living for the duration of training or studies\nunder the same conditions as German citizens. Furthermore, adults with a protection status are entitled\nto participate in the ‘integration courses’ which in their general form consist of 600 language lesson units\nand 100 lesson units in an ‘orientation course’ where participants are meant to learn about the legal\nsystem as well as history and culture in Germany and about ‘community life’ and ‘values that are important\nin Germany’.1581 Participants have to cover part of the costs themselves, unless they receive\nunemployment benefits or social assistance. Next to the general integration courses, there are special\ncourses e. g. courses for women or parents, literacy courses or intensive courses for experienced\nlearners.\n\n\n\n\n1578\n         Brücker, Herbert; Fendel, Tanja; Guichard, Lucas; Gundacker, Lidwina; Jaschke, Philipp; Keita, Sekou;\n         Kosyakova, Yuliya; Vallizadeh, Ehsan (2020): Fünf Jahre ‘Wir schaffen das’ - Eine Bilanz aus der Perspektive\n         des Arbeitsmarktes. Access to education\n\nPersons with refugee status and beneficiaries of subsidiary protection are entitled to take up vocational\ntraining as well as school or university education, if they can prove that they have the necessary\nqualifications. They can also receive support for the costs of living for the duration of training or studies\nunder the same conditions as German citizens. Furthermore, adults with a protection status are entitled\nto participate in the ‘integration courses’ which in their general form consist of 600 language lesson units\nand 100 lesson units in an ‘orientation course’ where participants are meant to learn about the legal\nsystem as well as history and culture in Germany and about ‘community life’ and ‘values that are important\nin Germany’.1581 Participants have to cover part of the costs themselves, unless they receive\nunemployment benefits or social assistance. Next to the general integration courses, there are special\ncourses e. g. courses for women or parents, literacy courses or intensive courses for experienced\nlearners.\n\n\n\n\n1578\n         Brücker, Herbert; Fendel, Tanja; Guichard, Lucas; Gundacker, Lidwina; Jaschke, Philipp; Keita, Sekou;\n         Kosyakova, Yuliya; Vallizadeh, Ehsan (2020): Fünf Jahre ‘Wir schaffen das’ - Eine Bilanz aus der Perspektive\n         des Arbeitsmarktes. (IAB-Forschungsbericht, 11/2020), Nuremberg.\n1579\n         Ibid. 24 ff.\n1580\n         Brücker, Herbert; Hauptmann, Andreas; Jaschke, Philipp (2020): Beschränkungen der Wohnortwahl für\n         anerkannte Geflüchtete: Wohnsitzauflagen reduzieren die Chancen auf Arbeitsmarktintegration. (IAB-\n         Kurzbericht, 03/2020), Nuremberg.\n1581\n         See BAMF, ‘The content and stages of the procedure’, available at: https://bit.ly/3fNqh1S.\n\n\n                                                         235\n\nAccording to the updated brief analysis mentioned in Access to the labour market, 33% of persons\nsurveyed (i.e. persons who arrived in Germany as asylum seekers between 2013 and 2019) had attended\none of the following educational institutions:1582\n    v Schools, further education: 12%;\n    v Vocational training institution: 18%;\n    v Universities, colleges: 5%.\nAs noted above, the study does not distinguish between the protection status (and/or the residence status)\nof people surveyed, but it can provide an indication to the situation of persons with protection status. More\nrecent data is not available.\n\nConcerning the access to higher education (more extensively discussed, see Access to education) while\nthere have been some improvements, the lack of sufficient language skills, discrimination and the\nrecognition of former degrees, continue to hinder access to higher education for beneficiaries of and\napplicants for international protection.\n\nFor refugees and beneficiaries of subsidiary protection, several options are available if they were not able\nto finish school neither in their country of origin nor in Germany. Some vocational trainings do not require\ngraduation from school. Most of these trainings are two-year trainings which require less theoretical skills.\nAfter the completion of the two-years training, there are in many cases career options through further\ntrainings available. Additional support programs designed for young immigrants shall facilitate the search\nfor adequate vocational trainings, support the integration in the labour market and in the vocational training\nitself and support companies who provide vocational trainings for young immigrants.1583 For example, the\n‘orientation program for refugees’ offers a 13 week program in which refugees and beneficiaries of\nsubsidiary protection acquire the language and skillset necessary for the vocational training they wish to\nstart.1584 In 2022, 1,045 people participated in the program.1585 26% of those completed the program\nstarted a vocational training afterwards. There are as well possibilities to complete school education after\nhaving dropped out of the regular school system. The exact programs depend on the Federal states. In\nmost states, the successful completion of a vocational training equalises lower school education and\nadditionally daytime or evening schools are available to catch up the school education.1586\n\nFor refugees and beneficiaries of subsidiary protection, the same support structures as for German\nnationals are available for families with young children. From the age of one year, the state is by law\nobliged to provide a place in a nursery or kindergarten.1587 However, since the introduction of the obligation\nthe state has been unable to provide enough nursery or kindergarten places. A study from 2023 concludes\nthat there is currently a lack of 430,000 places. For disadvantaged families e.g., refugee families, the\nFederal Ministry for Family Affairs, Senior Citizens, Women and Youth set up a programme to facilitate\naccess and integration to the German nursery and kindergarten system.1588 The programme includes the\ndissemination of information on the nursery system and aims to facilitate contact between families and\nnurseries or kindergartens.\n\n\n\n\n1582\n       Herbert, Brücker, Philipp Jaschke, Yuliya Kosyakova & Ehsan Vallizadeh, Entwicklung der\n       Arbeitsmarktintegration seit Ankunft in Deutschland: Erwerbstätigkeit und Löhne von Geflüchteten steigen\n       deutlich, 2023, available in German at: https://bit.ly/3fNqh1S.\n1583\n       For an overview: Stark für Ausbildung, Deutschlandweite Programme und Projekte für Junge Geflüchtete,\n       Zuwanderer, Migranten, last access 16 February 2024, available in German at: https://bit.ly/3uByh0S.\n1584\n       Federal Ministry of Education and Research, Vocational orientation – provision for refugees, last access 16\n       February 2024, available at: https://bit.ly/3UGRNUk.\n1585\n       Federal Ministry of Education and Research, BOF-Programm erreicht immer mehr Frauen – Unterstützung\n       auf dem Weg in eine Ausbildung bleibt wichtig, last access 16 Ferauary 2024, available in German at:\n       https://bit.ly/49zNIpg.\n1586\n       Planet-Beruf.de, Ausbildung ohne Schulabschluss - More\nrecent data is not available.\n\nConcerning the access to higher education (more extensively discussed, see Access to education) while\nthere have been some improvements, the lack of sufficient language skills, discrimination and the\nrecognition of former degrees, continue to hinder access to higher education for beneficiaries of and\napplicants for international protection.\n\nFor refugees and beneficiaries of subsidiary protection, several options are available if they were not able\nto finish school neither in their country of origin nor in Germany. Some vocational trainings do not require\ngraduation from school. Most of these trainings are two-year trainings which require less theoretical skills.\nAfter the completion of the two-years training, there are in many cases career options through further\ntrainings available. Additional support programs designed for young immigrants shall facilitate the search\nfor adequate vocational trainings, support the integration in the labour market and in the vocational training\nitself and support companies who provide vocational trainings for young immigrants.1583 For example, the\n‘orientation program for refugees’ offers a 13 week program in which refugees and beneficiaries of\nsubsidiary protection acquire the language and skillset necessary for the vocational training they wish to\nstart.1584 In 2022, 1,045 people participated in the program.1585 26% of those completed the program\nstarted a vocational training afterwards. There are as well possibilities to complete school education after\nhaving dropped out of the regular school system. The exact programs depend on the Federal states. In\nmost states, the successful completion of a vocational training equalises lower school education and\nadditionally daytime or evening schools are available to catch up the school education.1586\n\nFor refugees and beneficiaries of subsidiary protection, the same support structures as for German\nnationals are available for families with young children. A study from 2023 concludes\nthat there is currently a lack of 430,000 places. Social welfare\n\nBoth refugees and beneficiaries of subsidiary protection are entitled to social benefits, in particular\nunemployment benefits, on the same level as German nationals. There have been substantial reforms of\nthe legal framework governing social benefits in Germany through the so called 'citizens benefits law’\n(Bürgergeld Gesetz) which entered into force on 1 January 2023. They entail changes to social benefits\nwhich respectively apply for German nationals as well as for beneficiaries of international protection. By\nway of example, the amount of financial benefits has been lifted from € 449 to € 502 for single adults,\n€ 451 for spouses, children between 14 and 17 years € 420 and children between six and thirteen € 348\nand children under six years € 318. Additionally, grounds for penalties upon non-compliance with\nobligations to cooperate have been reduced and the amount of financial reserves and extra income next\nto the unemployment benefits has been raised.1589\n\nIn order to meet the late effects of the Covid-19 pandemic and inflation the Federal government further\nintroduced several ad hoc measures. Children receive a monthly support of € 20 to facilitate social and\nfinancial participation, adults who received unemployment benefits in June 2022 received an additional\nsum of € 200 for July 2022.1590\n\nBeneficiaries of international protection are entitled to benefits, starting from the first day of the month\nafter the recognition of their status has become legally valid i.e. usually with the arrival of the decision by\nthe BAMF. Problems with access to social benefits may occur during the period when persons have\nalready been granted protection status but still only have the asylum seeker’s permission to stay\n(Aufenthaltsgestattung) because they have not yet received the residence permit (Aufenthaltserlaubnis)\nwhich officially confirms that they have protection status. This may lead responsible authorities to deny\nsocial services for the first couple of weeks following the recognition of the status. However, persons\nconcerned would in any case be entitled to the (lower) asylum seekers’ benefits during this period and\nthey can claim payments to which they would have been entitled at a later date.1591\n\nFor persons who are registered as unemployed, the responsible authority is the job centre or Employment\nAgency. This institution is responsible for the disbursement of unemployment benefits as well as for the\nprovision of other benefits and measures for integration into the labour market; job training measures,\nsupport with job applications, specific language courses etc. For persons who are not registered as\nunemployed (e.g., because they have reached the age of retirement or are unable to work on health\ngrounds), the responsible authority is the Social Welfare Office.\n\nSince August 2016, beneficiaries of protection are generally obliged to take up their place of residence\nwithin the Federal State in which their asylum procedures have been conducted for a maximum period of\nthree years (see Freedom of movement). In these cases, social benefits are only provided in the\nrespective municipality.\n\n\n\n G. Health care\n\nPersons with refugee status and beneficiaries of subsidiary protection have the same status as German\ncitizens within the social insurance system. This includes membership in the statutory health insurance,\nif they have a job other than minimal employment (i.e., a low-paid part time job). If they are unemployed,\nthe job centre or the social welfare office provides them with a health insurance card which entitles them\nto the same medical care as statutory health insurance. Dezember 2024\nPflegebedürftige überwiegend zu Hause versorgt (86 %)\nDeutliche Zunahme der Zahl der Pflegebedürftigen um 15 % gegenüber dem Jahresende 2021 auch durch weiter gefassten Pflegebedürftigkeitsbegriff\n17 % mehr zu Hause gepflegte Personen; 1 % mehr vollstationär in Heimen versorgte Pflegebedürftige\nWIESBADEN – Im Dezember 2023 waren in Deutschland knapp 5,7 Millionen Menschen pflegebedürftig im Sinne des Pflegeversicherungsgesetzes (\nSGB\nXI\n). Wie das Statistische Bundesamt (Destatis) mitteilt, hatte die Zahl der Pflegebedürftigen im Dezember 2021 bei knapp 5,0 Millionen gelegen. Die starke Zunahme um 730 000 Pflegebedürftige (+15 %) weist darauf hin, dass sich hier auch noch Effekte durch die Einführung des weiter gefassten Pflegebedürftigkeitsbegriffs zum 1. Januar 2017 zeigen. Seither werden Menschen eher als pflegebedürftig eingestuft als zuvor\n.\nDie Zahl der Pflegebedürftigen wächst in stärkerem Maße, als durch die Alterung der Gesellschaft erwartbar ist. So wurde nach der\nPflegevorausberechnung\ndes Statistischen Bundesamtes der demografiebedingte Anstieg von 2021 bis 2023 auf rund 100 000 Pflegebedürftige beziffert. Die tatsächliche Zunahme der Zahl der Pflegebedürftigen um 730 000 geht zum Teil auf den weiter gefassten Pflegebegriff zurück.\nLädt...\nKnapp neun von zehn Pflegebedürftigen wurden zu Hause versorgt\nKnapp neun von zehn Pflegebedürftigen (86 % beziehungsweise 4,9 Millionen) wurden im Dezember 2023 zu Hause versorgt. Davon erhielten 3,1 Millionen Pflegebedürftige ausschließlich Pflegegeld und wurden überwiegend durch Angehörige gepflegt. Weitere 1,1 Millionen Pflegebedürftige lebten ebenfalls in Privathaushalten und wurden zusammen mit oder vollständig durch ambulante Pflege- und Betreuungsdienste versorgt. Ebenfalls zu Hause versorgt wurden weitere 680 000 Pflegebedürftige im Pflegegrad 1. Davon erhielten 40 000 ausschließlich Entlastungsleistungen landesrechtlicher Angebote. Die übrigen 640 000 Pflegebedürftigen im Pflegegrad 1 haben im Dezember 2023 keine Leistungen von Pflegeheimen oder ambulanten Diensten genutzt. Ob diese Personen weitere Hilfeleistungen abgerufen haben, wird in der Pflegestatistik nicht erfasst.\nEin Siebtel der Pflegebedürftigen (14 % beziehungsweise 800 000) wurde in Pflegeheimen vollstationär betreut.\nIm Vergleich zu Dezember 2021 stieg die Zahl der in Heimen vollstationär versorgten Pflegebedürftigen leicht um 0,8 % (+6 000). Die Zahl der zu Hause gepflegten Personen nahm dagegen insgesamt um gut ein Sechstel (+17 %) oder 721 000 zu. Davon stieg die Zahl der durch ambulante Dienste betreuten Pflegebedürftigen um 5,1 % (+54 000). Die Zahl der überwiegend durch Angehörige versorgten Pflegebedürftigen stieg um gut ein Fünftel (+21 %) oder 549 000. Ebenfalls um rund ein Fünftel (+21 % oder +118 000 Personen) wuchs die Gruppe der Pflegebedürftigen im Pflegegrad 1 ohne Leistungen ambulanter oder stationärer Einrichtungen beziehungsweise mit ausschließlich landesrechtlichen Entlastungsleistungen.\nEin Drittel der Pflegebedürftigen war 85 Jahre und älter\nEnde 2023 waren 78 % der Pflegebedürftigen 65 Jahre und älter, rund ein Drittel (34 %) war mindestens 85 Jahre alt. Die Mehrheit der Pflegebedürftigen war weiblich (61 %). Dies ist unter anderem darauf zurückzuführen, dass Frauen eine höhere Lebenserwartung haben und die Wahrscheinlichkeit, pflegebedürftig zu sein, mit zunehmendem Alter steigt. Während bei den 70- bis 74-Jährigen rund 11 % pflegebedürftig waren, betrug die Pflegequote für die ab 90-Jährigen 87 %.\n\nMethodische Hinweise:\nDie Leistungen im Pflegegrad 1 sind in\n§\n28a\nSGB\nXI\ngeregelt. Sie beinhalten neben den Leistungen der ambulanten Dienste und Pflegeheime oder der nach Landesrecht anerkannten Angebote zur Unterstützung im Alltag auch weitere Leistungen, die in der Pflegestatistik nicht betrachtet werden, wie zum Beispiel Beratung, Pflegekurse, Pflegehilfsmittel oder Verbesserungen des Wohnumfelds. Aufgrund des abweichenden Leistungsrechts erhalten Pflegebedürftige des Grades 1 kein Pflegegeld. Die Leistungen der Pflegeversicherung für Pflegebedürftige des Pflegegrades 1 konzentrieren sich somit darauf, die Selbstständigkeit der Betroffenen durch frühzeitige Hilfestellungen möglichst lange zu erhalten und ihnen den Verbleib in der vertrauten häuslichen Umgebung zu ermöglichen (siehe dazu auch die entsprechende\nThemenseite\nim Internetangebot des Bundesministeriums für Gesundheit).\nWeitere Informationen:\nWeitere Ergebnisse und Erläuterungen der zweijährlichen Statistik – insbesondere auch zu Pflegeheimen und ambulanten Pflegediensten einschließlich des Personals – stehen in der Datenbank GENESIS-\nOnline\n(Tabellen\n22411\n,\n22412\nund\n22421\n) sowie im Statistischen Bericht „\nPflegestatistik - Pflege im Rahmen der Pflegeversicherung 2023\n“ zur Verfügung.\nWichtiger Hinweis zur Datenbank GENESIS-\nOnline\n:\nSeit dem 5. November 2024 ist die neue Nutzeroberfläche unserer Datenbank als Hauptversion verfügbar und hat damit das Beta-Stadium verlassen. Februar 2024\nZahl genehmigter Wohnungen sinkt gegenüber dem Vorjahr um 94 100 auf 260 100 Wohnungen und damit auf den niedrigsten Stand seit 2012\n42,2 % weniger Wohnungsbauvorhaben von Privatpersonen und 20,3 % weniger von Unternehmen als im Vorjahr – besonders starke Rückgänge bei Ein- und Zweifamilienhäusern\nDeutliche Rückgänge auch bei Nichtwohngebäuden: Insgesamt 15,7 % weniger umbauter Raum als im Vorjahr, Minus von 20,9 % bei Büro- und Verwaltungsgebäuden\nWIESBADEN – Im Jahr 2023 wurde in Deutschland der Bau von 260 100 Wohnungen genehmigt. Wie das Statistische Bundesamt (Destatis) nach vorläufigen Ergebnissen mitteilt, waren das 26,6 % oder 94 100 Wohnungen weniger als im Jahr 2022. Niedriger war die Zahl der Baugenehmigungen zuletzt im Jahr 2012 (241 100 Wohnungen). In den Zahlen sind sowohl die Baugenehmigungen für Wohnungen in neuen Gebäuden als auch für neue Wohnungen in bestehenden Gebäuden enthalten. Die Zahl der Baugenehmigungen ist ein wichtiger Frühindikator für die zukünftige Bauaktivität, da Baugenehmigungen geplante Bauvorhaben darstellen. Zum Rückgang der Bauvorhaben im Jahr 2023 dürften unter anderem gestiegene\nKosten für Baumaterialien\nund verschlechterte Finanzierungsbedingungen beigetragen haben.\nLädt...\nGanzjährig negative Entwicklung im Vergleich zu den Vorjahresmonaten\nDie Zahl der Baugenehmigungen für Wohnungen war in allen Monaten des Jahres 2023 niedriger als im jeweiligen Vorjahresmonat. Mit negativen Veränderungsraten von über 30 % waren die Rückgänge in den Monaten April, Juli und August sowie zuletzt im Dezember 2023 (-35,1 % gegenüber Dezember 2022 auf 21 200 Wohnungen) besonders deutlich. Alle übrigen Monate bis auf Oktober und November 2023 (-10,7 % und -15,4 %) wiesen Rückgänge von über 20 % gegenüber dem jeweiligen Vorjahresmonat auf.\nNeubauwohnungen: Deutlich weniger Bauanträge von Privatpersonen\nIm Jahr 2023 wurden in neu zu errichtenden Wohngebäuden 214 100 Wohnungen genehmigt. Das waren 29,7 % oder 90 200 Neubauwohnungen weniger als im Vorjahr.\nRund 93 % der Bauanträge für Wohnungen in neuen Wohngebäuden werden in Deutschland von Unternehmen und Privatpersonen gestellt. Entsprechend prägen diese beiden Gruppen die Gesamtentwicklung. Auf Unternehmen entfielen 117 700 Baugenehmigungen für Wohnungen und damit 20,3 % oder 30 000 weniger als im Vorjahr. Auf Privatpersonen gingen 81 300 Baugenehmigungen zurück, das waren 42,2 % oder 59 400 weniger als im Jahr zuvor. Die Zahl der Baugenehmigungen, die auf Bauanträge der öffentlichen Hand zurückgehen, sank um 12,1 % (-1 500) auf 11 000 Bauanträge.\nStarke Rückgänge bei Ein- und Zweifamilienhäusern\nNach Gebäudearten betrachtet ging im Jahr 2023 die Zahl der Baugenehmigungen für Einfamilienhäuser (-39,1 % oder -30 500 auf 47 600) und Zweifamilienhäuser (-48,3 % oder -13 400 auf 14 300 Wohnungen) besonders stark zurück. Diese beiden Gebäudearten werden im Allgemeinen von Privatpersonen errichtet. Etwa zwei Drittel der Neubauwohnungen in Deutschland entstehen in Mehrfamilienhäusern, die überwiegend von Unternehmen gebaut werden. Hier sank die Zahl der Baugenehmigungen um 25,1 % oder 47 800 auf 142 600 Wohnungen.\nUmbauter Raum bei Fabrik- sowie Bürogebäuden ebenfalls deutlich gesunken\nAuch bei Nichtwohngebäuden gingen im Jahr 2023 die neuen Bauvorhaben deutlich zurück. Nichtwohngebäude sind zum Beispiel Fabrikgebäude und Lagerhallen, Büro- und Verwaltungsgebäude oder landwirtschaftliche Betriebsgebäude. Der zentrale Indikator zur Messung der Bauaktivität ist hier der umbaute Raum. Bei den genehmigten Nichtwohngebäuden verringerte sich der umbaute Raum gegenüber 2022 um 15,7 % auf 199,5 Millionen Kubikmeter. Das war der niedrigste Wert seit 2015 (194,0 Millionen Kubikmeter). Darunter ging der umbaute Raum bei Fabrik- und Werkstattgebäuden um 17,0 %, bei Warenlagern um 16,0 % und bei Handelsgebäuden um 23,3 % zurück.\nMit einem Minus von 20,9 % zum Vorjahr war bei den Büro- und Verwaltungsgebäuden im Jahr 2023 erneut ein starker Rückgang des umbauten Raums auf 18,0 Millionen Kubikmeter zu beobachten. Im Zeitraum von 2010 bis 2021 war der umbaute Raum bei dieser Gebäudeart von 10,8 Millionen auf 27,3 Millionen Kubikmeter angestiegen. Im Jahr 2022 kam es dann zu einem Einbruch auf 22,8 Millionen Kubikmeter, der sich im Jahr 2023 fortsetzte. Der Rückgang von 34,1 % seit 2021 dürfte unter anderem auf einen verminderten Bedarf an neuen Büro- und Verwaltungsgebäuden aufgrund des verstärkten Arbeitens im Homeoffice seit der Corona-Pandemie zurückzuführen sein.\nBaugenehmigungen als Indikator für Baufertigstellungen", "excerpt_sha256": "a2bc3e45547ab856b79d062a080200b4123e9fbcace4d5423474ce73ddf06b11", "verification_status": "accepted", "verification_notes": []}, {"evidence_id": "BATCH-0023-E012", "source_id": "SRC-f7c7d69225ebb851", "url": "https://www.destatis.de/DE/Presse/Pressemitteilungen/2024/04/PD24_147_63.html", "title": "Weiterhin gut ein Fünftel der Bevölkerung von Armut oder sozialer Ausgrenzung bedroht", "publisher": "Destatis", "raw_sha256": "dcd5f6a2da2286f6d8c2599d6feea477fc8a9d1477c247034fb1e9e2ef2a896e", "fact_summary": "In 2023, 17.7 million people in Germany, or 21.2% of the population, were at risk of poverty or social exclusion, nearly unchanged from 21.1% in 2022; 14.3% were at risk of poverty, 6.9% experienced severe material and social deprivation, and 9.8% of people under 65 lived in households with very low work intensity.", "question_ids": ["6B.10", "6B.2", "6B.3", "6B.6"], "polarity": "adverse", "period_fit": "Directly concerns Germany in 2023.", "ruler_attribution": "Indirect: the indicators describe social conditions during Scholz's chancellorship but do not identify which government policies caused them.", "limitations": ["The figures are first results and the income questions refer to the prior year's income.", "EU-SILC methodology changed in 2020, preventing direct comparison with earlier years."], "start_unit": 5, "end_unit": 6, "locator": "extracted HTML block 5 through extracted HTML block 6", "exact_excerpt": "Startseite\nPresse\nWeiterhin gut ein Fünftel der Bevölkerung von Armut oder sozialer Ausgrenzung bedroht\nPresse\nWeiterhin gut ein Fünftel der Bevölkerung von Armut oder sozialer Ausgrenzung bedroht\nSeite teilen\nPressemitteilung Nr. 147 vom 10. April 2024\n17,7 Millionen Betroffene in Deutschland im Jahr 2023\n14,3 % der Bevölkerung waren armutsgefährdet, 6,9 % waren von erheblicher materieller und sozialer Entbehrung betroffen, 9,8 % der Menschen lebten in einem Haushalt mit sehr geringer Erwerbsbeteiligung\nWIESBADEN – In Deutschland waren im Jahr 2023 gut 17,7 Millionen Menschen von Armut oder sozialer Ausgrenzung bedroht. Das waren 21,2 % der Bevölkerung, wie das Statistische Bundesamt (Destatis) anhand von Erstergebnissen der Erhebung zu Einkommen und Lebensbedingungen (EU-SILC) mitteilt. Gegenüber dem Vorjahr blieben die Werte nahezu unverändert. So waren im Jahr 2022 rund 17,5 Millionen Menschen oder 21,1 % der Bevölkerung von Armut oder sozialer Ausgrenzung bedroht.\nEine Person gilt in der Europäischen Union (EU) als\nvon Armut oder sozialer Ausgrenzung bedroht\n, wenn mindestens eine der folgenden drei Bedingungen zutrifft: Ihr Einkommen liegt unter der Armutsgefährdungsgrenze, ihr Haushalt ist von erheblicher\nmaterieller und sozialer Entbehrung\nbetroffen oder sie lebt in einem\nHaushalt mit sehr geringer Erwerbsbeteiligung\n. Für jede dieser Lebenssituationen kann jeweils der Anteil der Betroffenen an der Bevölkerung ermittelt werden.\nIm Jahr 2023 war etwa jede siebte Person (14,3 % der Bevölkerung oder knapp 12,0 Millionen Menschen) in Deutschland armutsgefährdet. Im Jahr 2022 hatte die\nArmutsgefährdungsquote\n14,8 % betragen. Nach EU-SILC gilt eine Person als armutsgefährdet, wenn sie über weniger als 60 % des mittleren\nÄquivalenzeinkommens\nder Gesamtbevölkerung verfügt. 2023 lag dieser Schwellenwert für eine alleinlebende Person in Deutschland netto (nach Steuern und Sozialabgaben) bei 1 310 Euro im Monat (Äquivalenzeinkommen), für zwei Erwachsene mit zwei Kindern unter 14 Jahren lag er bei 2 751 Euro im Monat. Um das Einkommen vollständig zu erfassen, wird das Jahreseinkommen erfragt. Dadurch beziehen sich die Fragen zum Einkommen auf das Vorjahr der Erhebung, in diesem Fall also auf das Jahr 2022.\n6,9 % der Bevölkerung oder 5,7 Millionen Menschen in Deutschland waren im Jahr 2023 von erheblicher materieller und sozialer Entbehrung betroffen (2022: 6,2 %). Das bedeutet, dass ihre Lebensbedingungen aufgrund von fehlenden finanziellen Mitteln deutlich eingeschränkt waren. Die Betroffenen waren zum Beispiel nicht in der Lage, ihre Rechnungen für Miete, Hypotheken oder Versorgungsleistungen zu bezahlen, eine einwöchige Urlaubsreise zu finanzieren, abgewohnte Möbel zu ersetzen oder einmal im Monat im Freundeskreis oder mit der Familie etwas essen oder trinken zu gehen.\n9,8 % der Bevölkerung unter 65 Jahren oder 6,2 Millionen Menschen in Deutschland lebten 2023 in einem Haushalt mit sehr niedriger Erwerbsbeteiligung (2022: 9,8 %). Das heißt, die Haushaltsmitglieder waren insgesamt sehr wenig oder nicht in den Arbeitsmarkt eingebunden. Nach EU-SILC liegt diese Situation vor, wenn die Erwerbsbeteiligung der erwerbsfähigen Haushaltsmitglieder im Alter von 18 bis 64 Jahren im Vorjahr der Erhebung insgesamt weniger als 20 % betrug. Dies war zum Beispiel der Fall, wenn in einem Haushalt mit zwei Personen in dieser Altersgruppe eine Person überhaupt nicht arbeitete und die andere insgesamt nur in vier von zwölf Monaten erwerbstätig war.\nEin EU-weiter Vergleich des Anteils der von Armut oder sozialer Ausgrenzung bedrohten Menschen an der Bevölkerung im Jahr 2023 ist zum jetzigen Zeitpunkt nicht möglich, da bislang erst gut die Hälfte der EU-Staaten ihre Ergebnisse veröffentlicht hat. Von den derzeit vorliegenden Ergebnissen war der Anteil in Tschechien im Jahr 2023 mit 12,0 % am niedrigsten, am höchsten war er in Bulgarien mit 30,0 %. Im Jahr 2022, für das ein Gesamtergebnis für die EU vorliegt, hatte Deutschland mit einem Anteil von 21,1 % knapp unter dem EU-Durchschnitt von 21,6 % gelegen.\n\nMethodische Hinweise:\nDie Ergebnisse entstammen der europäischen Gemeinschaftsstatistik über Einkommen und Lebensbedingungen (\nEuropean Union Statistics on Income and Living Conditions\n, EU-SILC). EU-SILC ist die amtliche Hauptdatenquelle für die Messung von Armutsgefährdung und Lebensbedingungen in Deutschland und der EU. In Deutschland ist die Erhebung seit dem Jahr 2020 als Unterstichprobe in den Mikrozensus integriert. Aufgrund der damit verbundenen umfangreichen methodischen Änderungen ist ein Vergleich der Ergebnisse ab 2020 mit den Vorjahren nicht möglich. Ausführliche Informationen hierzu bietet eine\nSonderseite\nim Internetangebot des Statistischen Bundesamtes.\nDamit zwischen dem Ende des Erhebungsjahres und der Ergebnisbereitstellung möglichst wenig Zeit vergeht, werden seit dem Erhebungsjahr 2020 zunächst Erstergebnisse und mit einigem zeitlichen Abstand Endergebnisse veröffentlicht. Bei den hier angegebenen Ergebnissen für 2023 handelt es sich um Erstergebnisse.\nIn der Erhebung EU-SILC ist die Grundlage für die Einkommensmessung in einem Erhebungsjahr das gesamte verfügbare Haushaltseinkommen (Einkommen nach Steuern und Sozialabgaben) des Vorjahres. Die Fragen zum Einkommen beziehen sich also auf das Vorjahr der Erhebung. Auch die Frage nach der Erwerbsbeteiligung bezieht sich auf das Vorjahr der Erhebung. Im Jahr 2023 wurden 35 428 Haushalte und 61 021 Personen ab 16 Jahren zu ihren Einkommen und Lebensbedingungen befragt.\nDie amtliche Statistik liefert zum Themenbereich \"Einkommen, Armutsgefährdung und soziale Lebensbedingungen\" Indikatoren auf Basis unterschiedlicher Datengrundlagen. Neben den hier vorgestellten Ergebnissen aus EU-SILC gibt es auch Daten zur Armutsgefährdung und Einkommensverteilung aus dem Mikrozensus-Kernprogramm. Diese dienen vor allem einer tieferen regionalen und fachlichen Untergliederung. N033 vom 1. Juli 2024\nKinder und Jugendliche von Eltern mit niedrigerem Bildungsabschluss überdurchschnittlich stark von Armut bedroht\n24 % der Kinder und Jugendlichen von Armut oder sozialer Ausgrenzung bedroht, Anteil liegt unter dem EU-Durchschnitt\nWIESBADEN – Knapp 2,1 Millionen Kinder und Jugendliche unter 18 Jahren in Deutschland waren 2023 armutsgefährdet. Das entspricht einer\nArmutsgefährdungsquote\nvon 14,0 %, wie das Statistische Bundesamt (Destatis) anhand von Ergebnissen der Erhebung zu Einkommen und Lebensbedingungen (EU-SILC) mitteilt. Die Armutsgefährdungsquote von Minderjährigen lag damit leicht unter der der Gesamtbevölkerung (14,4 %). Ähnlich wie letztere ist auch die Armutsgefährdungsquote von Kindern und Jugendlichen leicht rückläufig: 2022 hatte sie bei 15,0 % gelegen (Bevölkerung insgesamt 14,8 %).\nNach EU-SILC gilt eine Person als armutsgefährdet, wenn sie über weniger als 60 % des mittleren Äquivalenzeinkommens der Gesamtbevölkerung verfügt. 2023 lag dieser Schwellenwert für eine alleinlebende Person in Deutschland bei 1 314 Euro netto im Monat, für Haushalte mit zwei Erwachsenen mit zwei Kindern unter 14 Jahren waren es 2 759 Euro netto im Monat. Um das Einkommen vollständig zu erfassen, wird das Jahreseinkommen erfragt. Dadurch beziehen sich die Fragen zum Einkommen auf das Vorjahr der Erhebung, in diesem Fall also auf das Jahr 2022.\nKinder und Jugendliche von Eltern mit niedrigerem Bildungsabschluss besonders armutsgefährdet\nWie stark Kinder und Jugendliche von Armut bedroht sind, hängt auch von der Bildung ihrer Eltern ab. Die Armutsgefährdungsquote von unter 18-Jährigen, deren Eltern über einen niedrigeren Bildungsabschluss wie etwa einen Haupt- oder Realschulabschluss ohne beruflichen Abschluss verfügten, lag 2023 in Deutschland bei 36,8 %. Unter Kindern und Jugendlichen von Eltern mit einem mittleren Bildungsabschluss waren 14,3 % armutsgefährdet. Zu den mittleren Bildungsabschlüssen zählen beispielsweise eine abgeschlossene Berufsausbildung oder das Abitur.
